### F-OW-139 — First work as "little paintbrush": correcting a fellow novice (c. 8 Dec 1892)
- Claim: When Thérèse entered at 15 she found a fellow novice eight years older, "of a childlike character", who had entered a few months before; they became inseparable and the prioress allowed them spiritual talks. Thérèse was troubled that this sister's affection for the prioress was too human. Around 8 Dec 1892, after praying, she took the sister's head on her heart and told her, "with tears in my voice", everything she thought of her; the sister wept, agreed, and asked to be told of her faults always. Thérèse explains she herself, as a postulant, had such temptations to go to the prioress's room "to find a few drops of joy" that she had to hurry past and "cling to the banister".
- When / where: c. 8 Dec 1892, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Sr Marthe de Jésus (the fellow novice, not named in the text; identified by editors); Mère Marie de Gonzague
- Source: Ms C 20v–22r
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « j'étais obligée de passer rapidement devant le dépôt et de me cramponner à la rampe de l'escalier »
- Translation: "I had to hurry past the prioress's room and hang on to the banister."
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high for her text; medium for the identification.
- Notes: She compares an over-attached love to "the dog that attaches itself to its master".
### F-OW-140 — Uncle Guérin, Bluebeard and "Mirlitir"
- Claim: Thérèse reminded her uncle that 2 January was his birthday, and she was "proud to have been born on the same day". She would soon be "an old maid of twenty". She remembered him bouncing her on his knees while singing the ballad of Bluebeard "with terrible eyes that almost made me die of fright". She preferred the little tune of "Mirlitir": "the memory of that song is enough to make me laugh still". She signed "Your old Niece".
- When / where: 30 Dec 1892
- People: Isidore Guérin (pharmacist, her uncle); Mme Guérin
- Source: LT 139 (to M. and Mme Guérin, 30 Dec 1892), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-139-a-m-et-mme-guerin-30-decembre-1892/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Il me semble que c'était hier que mon bon Oncle me faisait sauter sur ses genoux en chantant la romance de Barbe-Bleue avec des yeux terribles qui me faisaient presque mourir de peur... Le petit air de Mirlitir était plus à mon goût... »
- Translation: "It seems like yesterday that my good Uncle bounced me on his knees singing the ballad of Bluebeard, with terrible eyes that almost made me die of fright… The little tune of Mirlitir was more to my taste…"
- Other sources: none
- New to English readers: yes ("romance de Barbe-Bleue", "vieille fille de vingt ans": ABSENT)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: This is a vivid childhood scene and a good example of her humour.
### F-OW-141 — Novice mistress in all but name: "I am a very small paintbrush"
- Claim: Mère Marie de Gonzague told her "Feed my lambs"; Thérèse said she was "too little" and asked the prioress to keep the novices herself; the prioress kept them but sent Thérèse to guide them. Thérèse: "I am a little paintbrush that Jesus has chosen to paint his image in the souls you entrusted to me"; the prioress is the big brush. "From far off it looks all rosy to do good to souls... From close up it is the opposite, the rose has gone." "What costs me above all is to observe their faults... and wage war to the death on them"; she thinks Jonah was "very excusable for running away". "I know your little lambs find me severe." Some novices said: "If you want anything from me, you must take me by gentleness; by force you'll get nothing."
- When / where: 1893–1897, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Mère Marie de Gonzague; the novices
- Source: Ms C 3v; 20r–24r
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « De loin cela paraît tout rose de faire du bien aux âmes... De près c'est tout le contraire, le rose a disparu »
- Translation: "From a distance it all looks rosy to do good to souls... Close up it's quite the opposite; the rose has gone."
- New to English readers: partial (baseline says she "helped with the novices")
- Confidence: high.
### F-OW-142 — "You have answers for everything": reading a novice's sorrow
- Claim: Novices said: "But you have an answer for everything... where do you get what you say?" Some thought she read their souls. One novice resolved to hide a sorrow; meeting her smiling, Thérèse said "You are unhappy." "If I had made the moon fall at her feet I don't think she'd have looked at me with more amazement." Thérèse felt "a supernatural fright" herself, being sure she had no gift of reading souls.
- When / where: 1893–1897, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: an unnamed novice
- Source: Ms C 26r
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « Si j'avais fait tomber la lune à ses pieds je crois qu'elle ne m'aurait pas regardée avec plus d'étonnement »
- Translation: "If I had made the moon drop at her feet, I don't think she could have looked at me with more astonishment."
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Also M-OW-08 (she presents it as unexplained insight, not a gift).
### F-OW-143 — A "good little salad with plenty of vinegar": being criticised by novices
- Claim: When her soul was tired of praise, Jesus served her "a good little salad, well vinegared, well spiced... nothing missing except the oil". The novices told her with great simplicity what displeased them in her; "it is a delicious feast". Once a novice satisfied her wish for humiliation so thoroughly that she thought of Shimei cursing David.
- When / where: 1893–1897, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: the novices
- Source: Ms C 27r (the Carmel site's transcription of 26v is empty; the start of the passage is in HA 1911 ch. X)
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « bien vinaigrée, bien épicée, rien n'y manque excepté l'huile »
- Translation: "well dressed with vinegar, well spiced, nothing missing but the oil"
- New to English readers: partial (in Taylor)
- Confidence: high.
### F-OW-145 — Pauline elected prioress: "Pauline became my living Jesus"
- Claim: "It was above all since the blessed day of your election that I flew in the ways of love... That day, Pauline became my living Jesus." She was set to paint "to the great astonishment of the sisters", took lessons from Pauline, and wrote poems and plays "that were found pretty".
- When / where: 20 Feb 1893 onward, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Mère Agnès de Jésus
- Source: Ms A 80v–81v
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « Ce jour-là, Pauline devint mon Jésus vivant... »
- Translation: "That day, Pauline became my living Jesus."
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high.
### F-OW-146 — Pauline becomes prioress: "a very little lyre"
- Claim: On the day Pauline was elected prioress, Thérèse wrote that she could now call her "Mother" openly. Before, it had been "in the secret of the heart". Mère Geneviève's spirit lived in her and her "prophetic word" was fulfilled: at thirty Pauline "began her public life", having written the life of Mère Geneviève for all the Carmels. Thérèse called her own heart "a very little lyre" that would sound at the touch of her "consecrated fingers".
- When / where: 20 Feb 1893, Carmel
- People: Mère Agnès de Jésus (Pauline Martin), newly elected prioress; Mère Geneviève (deceased)
- Source: LT 140 (to Mère Agnès de Jésus, 20 Feb 1893), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-140-a-mere-agnes-de-jesus-20-fevrier-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Petite Sœur, Mère chérie, mon cœur à moi, le cœur de votre enfant, est une toute petite lyre »
- Translation: "Little Sister, dear Mother, my own heart, the heart of your child, is a very little lyre."
- Other sources: baseline (Pauline prioress in 1893)
- New to English readers: yes for the letter. No for the bare fact of the election.
- Confidence: high
- Notes: She predicts: « Sans doute vous souffrirez... Les vases seront trop petits » ("No doubt you will suffer… The vessels will be too small").
### F-OW-147 — Céline as a hidden drop of dew
- Claim: Thérèse told Céline that simplicity was the mark of her heart. Céline should be "a drop of dew hidden in the corolla of the Lily of the valleys", not a loud river or a clear stream that people admire. "To be His, one must be small, small like a drop of dew!… Oh, how few souls aspire to stay small like that!"
- When / where: 25 Apr 1893, written after prayer "near the tabernacle"
- People: Céline
- Source: LT 141 (to Céline, 25 Apr 1893), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-141-a-celine-25-avril-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Pour être à Lui il faut être petit, petit comme une goutte de rosée !... Oh ! qu'il y a peu d'âmes qui aspirent à rester ainsi petites !... »
- Translation: "To be His, one must be small, small like a drop of dew!… Oh, how few souls long to stay small like that!…"
- Other sources: 1911 Lettre XIVe (25 Apr 1893)
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
- Notes: This is an early image of the "little way", two years before Ms A.
### F-OW-148 — She prayed that Céline would not dance; Céline's partner could only walk
- Claim: Before entering, Céline was invited to a ball; Thérèse, "against my habit", shed "a torrent of tears" and begged God to stop her dancing. Céline's partner "found himself totally unable to make her dance"; he could only walk her back to her seat, then slipped away and did not reappear all evening. Thérèse also writes that she could not accept Céline marrying: "it was impossible for me to see her give her heart to a mortal".
- When / where: c. 1892–1893 (the La Néele wedding party is suggested elsewhere; not stated here), Lisieux
- People: Céline; an unnamed dance partner
- Source: Ms A 82r
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « son cavalier se trouva dans l'impuissance totale de la faire danser »
- Translation: "her partner found himself completely unable to make her dance"
- Other sources: In the 1911 text (ch. VIII) with the dancing story; the line about "a mortal" is absent from the edited text.
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high as her report.
- Notes: Also listed as M-OW-07 (she presents it as an answered prayer).
### F-OW-149 — "My director, who is Jesus, does not teach me to count my acts"
- Claim: Thérèse told Céline that directors make people advance by having them make many acts of virtue, "and they are right". But "my director, who is Jesus", teaches her not to count her acts, but to do everything for love, in peace and abandonment: "it is Jesus who does everything and I do nothing". She said her way "is true only for very few souls".
- When / where: 6 July 1893
- People: Céline
- Source: LT 142 (to Céline, 6 July 1893), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-142-a-celine-6-juillet-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « les directeurs font avancer dans la perfection en faisant faire un grand nombre d'actes de vertu et ils ont raison, mais mon directeur qui est Jésus ne m'apprend pas à compter mes actes »
- Translation: "Directors make people advance in perfection by having them make a great many acts of virtue, and they are right. But my director, who is Jesus, does not teach me to count my acts."
- Other sources: 1911 Lettre XVIe uses other parts of LT 142 ("banque de l'amour") but not this passage.
- New to English readers: yes ("mon directeur qui est Jésus", "compter mes actes": ABSENT)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: This sets her apart from the common method of counting "practices", and she says so herself. Compare LT 144 (the "chapelet de pratiques").
### F-OW-150 — Out of ink, she spat in the inkwell
- Claim: At the end of a long spiritual letter, Thérèse wrote that Céline's heart alone would be able to read it, since she herself could hardly decipher it: "I have no more ink, I had to spit in our inkwell to make some… isn't that something to laugh at?"
- When / where: 6 July 1893, Carmel
- People: Céline
- Source: LT 142, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-142-a-celine-6-juillet-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « je n'ai plus d'encre, j'ai été obligée de cracher dans notre encrier pour en faire... n'y a-t-il pas de quoi rire ?... »
- Translation: "I have no more ink. I had to spit in our inkwell to make some… isn't that something to laugh at?…"
- Other sources: none
- New to English readers: yes ("cracher dans notre encrier": ABSENT)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: It shows poverty of means and her humour. "notre encrier": in Carmel, objects are "ours".
### F-OW-151 — "All five of us in our way": Céline chosen to stay with Papa
- Claim: After Léonie entered the Visitation, Thérèse wrote: "Now here we are, all five in our way." She told Céline she had not been "forgotten, less loved". She had been chosen "by privilege" to be "the crown, the reward" of their father, "the visible angel of our dear Father", while also being the bride of Jesus. She sent a kiss to "my dear king", from "his queen".
- When / where: 6 July 1893
- People: Céline; Louis Martin ("mon roi chéri"); Léonie (implied)
- Source: LT 142, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-142-a-celine-6-juillet-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Maintenant nous voilà toutes les 5 dans notre voie. » ; « J'embrasse toute la famille, mais surtout mon roi chéri qui recevra un baiser de sa Céline de la part de sa reine. »
- Translation: "Now here we are, all 5 of us, on our way." / "I kiss all the family, but above all my dear king, who will get a kiss from his Céline on behalf of his queen."
- Other sources: LT 169 ("sa pauvre petite reine"); Ms A (the father called her his little queen)
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The letter shows that Céline felt left behind. Thérèse's words answer that feeling.
### F-OW-152 — Opening the Gospel at random for Céline
- Claim: After reading Céline's letter, Thérèse went to prayer. Opening the Gospel, she asked Jesus to find a passage for Céline and "drew" the words about the fig tree: when you see these things, know the kingdom of God is near. "I closed the book, I had read enough."
- When / where: 18 July 1893, during prayer
- People: Céline; "Notre Mère" asked her to add a note to her letter
- Source: LT 143 (to Céline, 18 July 1893), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-143-a-celine-18-juillet-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « en prenant l'évangile j'ai demandé à Jésus de trouver un passage pour toi et voici ce que j'ai tiré » ; « J'ai fermé le livre, j'en avais assez lu »
- Translation: "Taking the Gospel, I asked Jesus to find a passage for you, and here is what I drew…" / "I closed the book. I had read enough."
- Other sources: none
- New to English readers: yes (this opening is cut in 1911, where the rest of LT 143 is merged into Lettre XVIe)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: This is a devotional practice of opening Scripture at random. See also the MIRACLES list as a reported sign.
### F-OW-153 — Little straws on the fire: a smile, a kind word
- Claim: Thérèse said God "no longer asks me anything", whereas at first He asked "an infinity of things". Then she had a light: St Teresa says love must be fed. In darkness, when there is no wood, we can still throw "little straws" on the fire. For example, "a smile, a kind word, when I would like to say nothing or look bored". "I am not always faithful, but I never get discouraged."
- When / where: 18 July 1893
- People: Céline; St Teresa of Ávila (cited)
- Source: LT 143, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-143-a-celine-18-juillet-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « par exemple un sourire, une parole aimable, alors que j'aurais envie de ne rien dire ou d'avoir l'air ennuyé » ; « je ne suis pas toujours fidèle, mais je ne me décourage jamais »
- Translation: "for example a smile, a kind word, when I would like to say nothing or to look bored" / "I am not always faithful, but I never get discouraged"
- Other sources: 1911 Lettre XVIe (undated) merges this with LT 142 and LT 147.
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
- Notes: See the edit card on the 1911 Lettre XVIe composite.
### F-OW-154 — The "rosary of practices" she made for a companion
- Claim: Thérèse told Céline she was now obliged to use "a rosary of practices" (a bead string for counting small sacrifices). She did it "out of charity for one of my companions". "I'll tell you about it in detail, it is rather funny… I am caught in nets that I do not like, but that are very useful to me in my present state of soul."
- When / where: 23 July 1893
- People: Céline; an unnamed companion in the novitiate
- Source: LT 144 (to Céline, 23 July 1893), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-144-a-celine-23-juillet-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Je suis même obligée d'avoir un chapelet de pratiques, je l'ai fait par charité pour une de mes compagnes, je te dirai cela en détail, c'est assez amusant... Je suis prise dans des filets qui ne me plaisent pas »
- Translation: "I even have to keep a rosary of practices. I did it out of charity for one of my companions. I will tell you the details, it is rather funny… I am caught in nets I do not like."
- Other sources: LT 142 (she does not count her acts)
- New to English readers: yes ("chapelet de pratiques": ABSENT)
- Confidence: high for the words. The companion is not named.
- Notes: Placed next to LT 142, this shows that she used counting beads unwillingly and for another's sake.
### F-OW-155 — Jesus asleep in Céline's boat
- Claim: Thérèse compared Céline to a small child alone in a boat at night on the open sea. Jesus sleeps in the boat, as He did in Galilee, and His pillow is Céline's heart. "Jesus would no longer sleep, and He is so tired!" She was touched that Jesus had inspired in Céline the idea of "little sacrifices", which she had asked Him for: "Never yet has Our Lord refused me to inspire you with what I had asked Him to tell you."
- When / where: 23 July 1893
- People: Céline
- Source: LT 144, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-144-a-celine-23-juillet-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Jamais Notre Seigneur ne m'a encore refusé de t'inspirer ce que je L'avais prié de te dire. »
- Translation: "Never yet has Our Lord refused to inspire you with what I had asked Him to tell you."
- Other sources: Ms A (Jesus asleep in her boat)
- New to English readers: yes (LT 144 does not appear in the 1911 Céline letters)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: She reports this as a pattern of answered prayer (see MIRACLES).
### F-OW-156 — La Musse: her "beautiful handwriting", Uncle's tired arm, Papa "like Job"
- Claim: Writing to Mme Guérin at the château de La Musse, Thérèse joked that she did not want "my beautiful handwriting" to lose the honour of being admired there. She prayed for her uncle's cure: his arm "never stops tiring itself writing admirable pages" that must save souls and "make the devils tremble". She prayed that Jeanne's "nasty sprain" would go. She teased Marie, who lived "in the heights of the trees". She rejoiced that her father was among them, "loaded with tenderness and care": God did for him as for Job, humbling him and then filling him with favours through them.
- When / where: 10 Aug 1893; the family at La Musse (the Guérins' country house)
- People: Mme Guérin; Isidore Guérin; Jeanne and Francis La Néele; Marie Guérin; Louis Martin; Mère Marie de Gonzague; "notre Mère" (Agnès); Madame de Virville
- Source: LT 146 (to Mme Guérin, 10 Aug 1893), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-146-a-mme-guerin-10-aout-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « je ne veux pas cependant que ma belle écriture perde l'honneur d'être admirée au château de La Musse ! » ; « le bras de mon bon Oncle ne cesse de se fatiguer à écrire des pages admirables qui doivent sauver les âmes et faire trembler les démons »
- Translation: "Still, I do not want my beautiful handwriting to lose the honour of being admired at the château of La Musse!" / "my good Uncle's arm never stops tiring itself writing admirable pages that must save souls and make the devils tremble"
- Other sources: LT 178 (Uncle as a saint)
- New to English readers: yes ("belle écriture": ABSENT)
- Confidence: high for the words. The letter does not say what the uncle was writing. His Catholic journalism is outside knowledge.
- Notes: She ends the letter written across the page and apologises that this is "not respectful".
### F-OW-157 — Céline's dishonest maid
- Claim: Céline had "miseries" with her maid ("ta bonne"). Thérèse wrote that the maid had "such a nasty fault, above all being false". Perhaps Céline could convert her, "as she did her husband". "Perhaps in her place I would be even less good than she, and perhaps she would already be a great saint if she had received half the graces God has showered on me." About "the house", Céline should rely on their uncle.
- When / where: 13 Aug 1893. "Notre Mère" allowed Thérèse to write because Céline was sad.
- People: Céline; Céline's unnamed maid and the maid's husband; Isidore Guérin; Mère Agnès (as prioress)
- Source: LT 147 (to Céline, 13 Aug 1893), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-147-a-celine-13-aout-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Ta pauvre bonne est bien malheureuse d'avoir un si vilain défaut, surtout d'être fausse » ; « peut-être à sa place serais-je encore moins bonne qu'elle »
- Translation: "Your poor maid is very unfortunate to have such an ugly fault, above all to be false." / "Perhaps in her place I would be even less good than she is."
- Other sources: 1911 Lettre XVIe keeps the peach passage from this letter and cuts the maid.
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
- Notes: This concerns household life at the time Céline was keeping house for her father. "La maison" (which house) is not explained.
### F-OW-158 — The peach: sweetness for us, the stone is its being
- Claim: Thérèse used a peach as an image. God did not make its velvety pink skin and its sugar for the peach but for us. Its essence is its stone. So Jesus showers gifts on some souls to draw others, then strips them. They feel good for nothing, but "the essence of their being works in secret". He uses "the vilest instruments" to show it is He alone who works.
- When / where: 13 Aug 1893
- People: Céline
- Source: LT 147, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-147-a-celine-13-aout-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Voici une belle pêche, rosée et si sucrée que tous les confiseurs ne sauraient imaginer une si douce saveur. »
- Translation: "Here is a beautiful peach, pink and so sweet that no confectioner could imagine so sweet a taste."
- Other sources: 1911 Lettre XVIe
- New to English readers: partial. It is in 1911 but changed (see the edit card).
- Confidence: high
- Notes: none
### F-OW-159 — EDIT: 1911 "Lettre XVIe" is a composite, and its peach ending is new
- Claim: 1911 "Lettre XVIe" (no date) combines LT 147 (the peach, 13 Aug 1893), LT 143 (the little straws, 18 July 1893) and LT 142 (the bank of love, the flock in the desert, 6 July 1893). In the peach passage, the original ends with Jesus using "the vilest instruments" and a final "Face à Face éternel". 1911 replaces this with a new image: souls "clothed in immortal beauty" to be "served without danger at the table of heaven". The original's "Ta Thérèse ne se trouve pas dans les hauteurs" moves position. 1911 puts it in the first person ("je m'abandonne dans les bras du Seigneur"), where the original speaks of "Thérèse" in the third person for the bank of love.
- When / where: originals July–Aug 1893; 1911 Lettre XVIe à Céline
- People: Mère Agnès (editor)
- Source: LT 142, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-142-a-celine-6-juillet-1893/ ; LT 143, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-143-a-celine-18-juillet-1893/ ; LT 147, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-147-a-celine-13-aout-1893/ ; 1911 Lettre XVIe
- Source type: own-writing compared with the edited text
- Rights: public-domain; Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: 1911: « elles seront servies sans danger sur la table des cieux » (not in LT 147) / LT 147: « Il se sert pour arriver à elles des instruments les plus vils »
- Translation: 1911: "they will be served without danger on the table of heaven" / LT 147: "to reach them He uses the vilest instruments"
- Other sources: 1911 text read directly
- New to English readers: yes (the difference)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: Taylor 1912 translated this composite, so English readers met a text that she never wrote as one letter.
### F-OW-160 — Léonie at the Visitation: "I shall not see you again on earth"
- Claim: Thérèse greeted Léonie's entry to the Visitation (in Caen). Like the dove from the ark, she had flown a long time looking for a place. "The prediction of our holy Aunt" was fulfilled. Thérèse's joy was "all spiritual", since she would never see Léonie again on earth. In heaven their mother would enjoy "her five religious daughters" and, with "the four little angels" waiting above, they would form a crown for their parents. Their father would be honoured "above all for the humiliations he was made to drink".
- When / where: 13 Aug 1893
- People: Léonie Martin; "notre sainte Tante" (Sr Marie-Dosithée Guérin, Visitandine of Le Mans; the name is from outside knowledge); Louis and Zélie Martin; the four Martin children who died young; the Superior of the Caen Visitation; Mère Agnès; Mère Marie de Gonzague; Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur
- Source: LT 148 (to Léonie, 13 Aug 1893), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-148-a-leonie-13-aout-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Elle est donc maintenant réalisée la prédiction de notre sainte Tante. » ; « nous jouirons de son bonheur en contemplant ses cinq filles religieuses et nous formerons avec les quatre petits anges qui nous attendent là-haut une couronne »
- Translation: "So now our holy Aunt's prediction has come true." / "We will enjoy her happiness as she looks at her five daughters in religion, and with the four little angels who wait for us above we will form a crown."
- Other sources: 1911 "Lettres à Sœur Françoise-Thérèse", Lettre Ire (13 Aug 1893)
- New to English readers: partial. The start is in 1911, but the prediction, the five daughters, the four little angels and the father's humiliations are cut.
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The file reads « L'enfant de la Breuse Marg. Marie est à la Visitation ». This looks like a transcription slip, probably for "Bienheureuse Marguerite-Marie". Check it against the manuscript. Léonie's first attempt at the Caen Visitation (1887) is not mentioned.
### F-OW-161 — EDIT: 1911 hides Léonie's name and builds a composite "first letter"
- Claim: 1911 prints Thérèse's letters to Léonie under Léonie's later religious name, "Sœur Françoise-Thérèse", without saying they were written to Léonie while she was trying out the Visitation. "Lettre Ire, 13 août 1893" combines LT 148 (13 Aug 1893), a sentence from LT 154 (27 Dec 1893: "Ici-bas la parole est impuissante"), the oak-tree comparison from LT 151 (5 Nov 1893, rewritten as "comme on nous le disait dans une instruction"), and the closing prayer of LT 163 (20 May 1894). "Lettre IIe, Janvier 1895" combines LT 173 (Jan 1895), LT 154 (Dec 1893: "demande-lui qu'elle reste toujours petite") and LT 164 (22 May 1894: "Laquelle des Thérèse… Ramasser une épingle par amour"). 1911 also cuts « peut-être ne verrons-nous pas finir l'année qui commence ! » from LT 173.
- When / where: originals 1893–95; 1911 edition
- People: Léonie (later Sr Françoise-Thérèse, Visitation of Caen); Mère Agnès (editor)
- Source: LT 148, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-148-a-leonie-13-aout-1893/ ; LT 151, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-151-a-leonie-5-novembre-1893/ ; LT 154, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-154-a-leonie-27-decembre-1893/ ; LT 163, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-163-a-soeur-therese-dosithee-leonie-20-mai-1894/ ; LT 164, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-164-a-soeur-therese-dosithee-leonie-22-mai-1894/ ; LT 173, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-173-a-soeur-therese-dosithee-leonie-janvier-1895/ ; 1911 Lettres à Sœur Françoise-Thérèse I–II

### F-OW-162 — "Friendly voices" warning Céline, and St Cecilia
- Claim: For Céline's feast in 1893 (she calls her "ma petite Soeur Marie de la Ste Face"), Thérèse wrote about "friendly voices" warning them "not to walk with too much security". She asked, "why fear the storm when the sky is clear?" But she accepted that Jesus wills it. The "flowers of the road" (pure pleasures of life) are not wrong, but Jesus wants all pleasures mixed with bitterness. She held up St Cecilia, who "sang in her heart" during her wedding music.
- When / where: 20 (?) Oct 1893 (date uncertain in the header)
- People: Céline; unnamed "voix amies"
- Source: LT 149 (to Céline, 20 (?) Oct 1893), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-149-a-celine-20-octobre-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Il fait retentir à nos oreilles des voix amies, ces voix nous avertissent de ne pas marcher avec trop de sécurité... » ; « Les fleurs du chemin ce sont les plaisirs purs de la vie, il n'y a aucun mal à en jouir »
- Translation: "He makes friendly voices sound in our ears. These voices warn us not to walk with too much security…" / "The flowers along the road are the pure pleasures of life. There is nothing wrong in enjoying them."
- Other sources: 1911 Lettre XVIIe (20 Oct 1893) keeps Cecilia and the lyre, and cuts the "voix amies" and "flowers of the road" passages.
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high for the words. Low for the occasion. The letter does not say what worried the "friendly voices". It may concern Céline's social outings (a ball is often mentioned in biographies), but this letter does not say so.
- Notes: The 1911 Lettre XVIIe also attaches the "petits poissons"/humility paragraph, which comes from LT 161 (26 Apr 1894). It is another composite.
### F-OW-163 — Jam, St Teresa's sardine, and praying to be an aunt
- Claim: Thanking Jeanne for jam and good wishes, Thérèse quoted St Teresa, who said pleasantly "that one won her heart with a sardine". She asked St Teresa (with Ste Anne) to make her an aunt: to send Jeanne a family that would give the Church "great Saints". "The delay does not discourage me, for I know the court of Rome needs a lot of time to make Saints."
- When / where: 22 Oct 1893
- People: Jeanne La Néele; Francis La Néele; St Teresa of Ávila (quoted)
- Source: LT 150 (to Mme La Néele, 22 Oct 1893), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-150-a-mme-la-neele-22-octobre-93/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Notre Mère Ste Thérèse était si reconnaissante qu'elle disait agréablement «qu'on lui gagnait le coeur avec une sardine». » ; « je sais qu'il faut bien du temps à la cour de Rome pour faire des Saints »
- Translation: "Our Mother St Teresa was so grateful that she used to say, pleasantly, 'that one could win her heart with a sardine'." / "I know the court of Rome needs a lot of time to make Saints."
- Other sources: LT 152 ("le grand Saint et le grand Pontife"); LT 180
- New to English readers: yes ("sardine": ABSENT)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: There is an irony here: she jokes about slow canonisations, and her own took 28 years.
### F-OW-164 — Retreat before St Teresa's feast: forest oaks and field oaks
- Claim: Thérèse told Léonie about a preacher's image from the retreat before St Teresa's feast. Oaks in the countryside grow crooked, branching left and right, and never grow tall. Forest oaks, pressed on all sides, see light only from above and grow very high. So the religious soul is pressed by the Rule. She also said she would rather let her pen run with her heart than "turn my phrases well".
- When / where: 5 Nov 1893
- People: Léonie; "le bon Père" (retreat preacher, not named)
- Source: LT 151 (to Léonie, 5 Nov 1893), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-151-a-leonie-5-novembre-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « j'aime mieux laisser courir ma plume sous l'impression de mon cœur que de bien tourner mes phrases » ; « Il n'a pas souffert qu'aucun mortel devienne l'époux d'une seule d'entre nous. »
- Translation: "I prefer to let my pen run under the impression of my heart rather than to turn my phrases well." / "He did not allow any mortal to become the husband of a single one of us."
- Other sources: 1911 Léonie Lettre Ire (reworded as an "instruction")
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
- Notes: none
### F-OW-165 — "My God, you know that I love you": her only prayer at the tabernacle
- Claim: Thérèse told her aunt that when she is near the tabernacle she can say only one thing to Our Lord: "My God, you know that I love you." She feels this prayer does not tire Jesus. Knowing His little bride's powerlessness, He is content with her good will.
- When / where: 17 Nov 1893
- People: Mme Guérin; Mme Fournet (Mme Guérin's mother, "Bonne-Maman")
- Source: LT 152 (to Mme Guérin, 17 Nov 1893), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-152-a-mme-guerin-17-novembre-1893/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Quand je suis auprès du Tabernacle je ne sais dire qu'une seule chose à Notre Seigneur : «Mon Dieu, vous savez que je vous aime.» »
- Translation: "When I am near the Tabernacle I can say only one thing to Our Lord: 'My God, you know that I love you.'"
- Other sources: her last words ("Mon Dieu, je vous aime") are in the baseline
- New to English readers: yes (ABSENT in 1911)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: This is a prayer habit, stated in 1893, that anticipates her last words.
### F-OW-166 — Mère Agnès ill; Mère Marie de Gonzague nurses "her little Prioress"
- Claim: Thérèse told her uncle that "Our Mother" (Agnès) was much better but very weak, "whatever she says". She thanked him for his care and hoped Agnès would obey "so paternal an Uncle". Mère Marie de Gonzague, touched by his attentions, was giving "all her motherly care to her dear little Prioress". "I am in such a hurry that I don't know what I am saying." She signed for "your three little Carmelites".
- When / where: Dec (?) 1893
- People: Isidore Guérin; Mère Agnès de Jésus (prioress, ill); Mère Marie de Gonzague (former prioress); Mme Fournet

- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Mère M. de Gonz. est bien touchée de vos attentions, elle vous remercie en donnant tous ses soins maternels à sa chère petite Prieure. »
- Translation: "Mother Marie de Gonzague is very touched by your kindness. She thanks you by giving all her motherly care to her dear little Prioress."
- Other sources: none
- New to English readers: yes

### F-OW-167 — Life of St Chantal read in the refectory
- Claim: Thérèse told Léonie that the refectory reading was the life of St Jane Frances de Chantal. It drew her closer to "the dear Visitation that I love so much", and showed the close union between the Visitation and the Carmel. She asked Léonie to pray "that I remain always little, very little!" She knew humility was Léonie's favourite virtue.
- When / where: 27 Dec 1893, Carmel refectory
- People: Léonie

- Confidence: high

### F-OW-168 — "My cat's handwriting" and St Teresa: "you treat them so badly"
- Claim: With only a few minutes before the letter was taken, and "the hour of Matins" come, Thérèse asked pardon for "my cat's handwriting". She quoted St Teresa: "My God, I am not surprised you have so few friends, you treat them so badly." She called her father's illness "the greatest cross I could have imagined", softened by the Guérins' hands.
- When / where: 29 Dec 1893
- People: Isidore and Mme Guérin; Louis Martin; Mme Fournet

- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown

- Translation: "This cross was the greatest I could have imagined." / "Here is the hour of Matins. Forgive the disorder of my letter and my cat's handwriting."
- Other sources: LT 178 repeats the St Teresa saying

### F-OW-169 — "The Dream of the Child Jesus": her painting for Pauline's feast
- Claim: For Mère Agnès's first feast as prioress, Thérèse painted a picture with a text: the Child Jesus asleep with flowers, dreaming of the cross, the lance, the crown of thorns and His disfigured face, and smiling. The flowers stand for Pauline's hidden virtues. She wrote that only Pauline's "artist's brush" could have painted such a mystery.
- When / where: 21 Jan 1894, Carmel
- People: Mère Agnès de Jésus (Pauline), who was herself a painter
- Source: LT 156 (to Mère Agnès de Jésus, 21 Jan 1894), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-156-a-mere-agnes-de-jesus-21-janvier-1894/

- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Vous venez de lire le rêve que votre enfant voulait reproduire pour votre fête. Mais hélas ! c'est votre pinceau d'artiste qui seul aurait pu peindre un aussi doux mystère !... »
- Translation: "You have just read the dream your child wanted to show for your feast. But alas! only your artist's brush could have painted so sweet a mystery!…"
- Other sources: LT 160 (the story of the painting)
- New to English readers: yes ("rêve de l'Enfant": ABSENT)

- Claim: Céline sent codfish ("codfiches") that pleased "Our Mother", who wanted to thank "her dear Célin" but could not write. Thérèse told Céline, now "without shelter", that Jesus looks at her "through the window" of His little house, and one day will say "Now it is my turn".

- People: Céline; Mère Agnès (as prioress); Marie Guérin (a letter from "Marie")

### F-OW-171 — Léonie admitted to the habit; "God is never outdone in generosity"
- Claim: Thérèse rejoiced that Léonie was accepted for the clothing. Before entering, Léonie had told her in the parlour that she would not mind always being last and taking the habit without ceremony. Thérèse quoted their father's frequent saying: "God never lets Himself be outdone in generosity."

### F-OW-172 — Céline Maudelonde's engagement, and a lottery of Chinese objects from Saigon
- Claim: Thérèse congratulated her cousin and childhood friend Céline Maudelonde on her engagement. She wrote that "the grilles of the Carmel are not made to separate hearts". She hoped Céline would bring her future husband's soul closer to Our Lord. In a P.S. she said the Prioress of the Saigon Carmel had sent many Chinese objects, including a "charming little parlour cabinet". "Our Mother" was raffling them for the community, at 0.50 francs a ticket.
- When / where: 26 Mar 1894
- People: Céline Maudelonde (later Mme Gaston Pottier); Hélène Maudelonde (her sister, ill); M. and Mme Maudelonde; the Prioress of the Saigon Carmel; Mère Agnès; Mère Marie de Gonzague
- Source: LT 159 (to Céline Maudelonde, 26 Mar 1894), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-159-a-celine-maudelonde-26-mars-1894/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Les grilles du Carmel ne sont pas faites pour séparer des cœurs qui ne s'aiment qu'en Jésus » ; « les billets sont à 0 F 50, nous en offrons à toutes les personnes amies de notre Carmel »
- Translation: "The grilles of the Carmel are not made to separate hearts that love each other only in Jesus." / "Tickets are 0.50 francs. We are offering them to all the friends of our Carmel."

- New to English readers: yes ("billets sont à": ABSENT)
- Confidence: high

### F-OW-173 — She wept on the painting: "My Jesus almost always sleeps"
- Claim: Thérèse told Sr Marie-Aloysia Vallée at the Le Mans Visitation that her "clumsy brush" could not show what her soul had dreamed. "I watered with my tears the white robe of my little Jesus." She decided to say nothing of the thought behind it, until Mother Agnès's kindness led her to tell her secret. The painting was then sent to Le Mans. She painted Jesus asleep because "He almost always sleeps… The Jesus of poor Thérèse does not caress her as He caressed her holy Mother." She remembered perfectly her visit to the Le Mans Visitation "at the age of 3".
- When / where: 3 Apr 1894
- People: Sr Marie-Aloysia Vallée (Visitation of Le Mans, whom she calls "Tante" and who helped form Marie and Pauline, "the two visible Angels who served me as true Mothers"); Mère Agnès; Sr Joseph de Sales
- Source: LT 160 (to Sr Marie-Aloysia Vallée, 3 Apr 1894), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-160-a-soeur-marie-aloysia-vallee-3-avril-1894/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « j'ai arrosé de mes larmes la robe blanche de mon petit Jésus » ; « En effet Il dort presque toujours... Le Jésus de la pauvre Thérèse ne la caresse pas comme Il caressait sa Ste Mère. » ; « Je me souviens parfaitement de mon voyage à la Visitation du Mans à l'âge de 3 ans »
- Translation: "I watered the white robe of my little Jesus with my tears." / "In fact He almost always sleeps… The Jesus of poor Thérèse does not caress her as He caressed her holy Mother [Teresa]." / "I remember perfectly my trip to the Visitation of Le Mans at the age of 3."
- Other sources: LT 156
- New to English readers: yes ("dort presque toujours": ABSENT)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: This is a frank statement of spiritual dryness in 1894, and of disappointment with her own artwork.
### F-OW-174 — A poem and a tiny painted picture for Céline's 25th birthday
- Claim: For Céline's 25th birthday, Thérèse sent "a little poem I composed thinking of you", on St Cecilia, "the Saint of abandonment". She recalled a dream Céline had as a child: an angel carrying a torch walking before their father. With it she sent a tiny picture she had coloured herself: « Jésus, qui vous a fait si petit ? l'amour. » The envelope read: painted "by little Thérèse… with the permission of the little Mother Prioress". She wrote: "if you can read me it is quite extraordinary".

- People: Céline; Louis Martin; Mère Agnès ("La Petite Mère Prieure")

- Translation: "You saw an angel carrying a torch, walking in front of our dear Father." / "Jesus, who made you so small? Love." / "Little picture painted by little Thérèse for little Céline's 25th birthday, with the permission of the little Mother Prioress"

### F-OW-175 — Léonie clothed as Sr Thérèse-Dosithée: "Which of the Thérèses will be more fervent?"
- Claim: Céline had told the Carmel "the smallest details" of Léonie's clothing on 6 April. Thérèse was glad that St Teresa had become Léonie's patron too. After elections at the Visitation, Léonie lost the Mother she loved. Two days later Thérèse asked: "Which of the Thérèses will be the more fervent?… the humblest". She added: "picking up a pin for love can convert a soul".

### F-OW-176 — Fear that Léonie will leave; "write a funny letter for everyone"
- Claim: A letter from Léonie "worries us very much… how unhappy she will be if she returns to the world!" Thérèse hoped it was only a temptation. "Our Mother is in great retreat." After pages on dryness (the "chariots of Aminadab"), Thérèse told Céline not to answer with a long letter about her soul: "I prefer you to write a very amusing letter for everyone. God wants me to forget myself, to give pleasure." She smiled at "my dear Papa" and kept him "through his visible angel".
- When / where: 7 July 1894
- People: Céline; Léonie; Mère Agnès; the Guérins; Louis Martin
- Source: LT 165 (to Céline, 7 July 1894), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-165-a-celine-7-juillet-1894/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Ah ! qu'elle sera malheureuse si elle revient dans le monde ! » ; « j'aime mieux que tu écrives une lettre bien amusante pour tout le monde. Le bon Dieu veut que je m'oublie, pour faire plaisir. »
- Translation: "Ah! how unhappy she will be if she comes back into the world!" / "I would rather you wrote a really amusing letter for everyone. God wants me to forget myself, to give pleasure."
- Other sources: 1911 Lettre XVIIIe (7 July 1894) keeps the Aminadab passage and cuts Léonie and the P.S.
- New to English readers: partial

### F-OW-177 — Céline Maudelonde's marriage: "our religion… enlarges hearts"
- Claim: Thérèse wrote to her newly married cousin that religion, "instead of narrowing hearts (as the world believes)", raises them and makes them able to love with an almost infinite love that lasts after death. She had asked Our Lady of Mount Carmel for the grace Céline obtained at Lourdes. She was happy Céline now wore the holy scapular. She prayed for the husband, that Jesus would "always change water into wine".
- When / where: 16 July 1894
- People: Mme Gaston Pottier (née Céline Maudelonde); M. Pottier; Mère Marie de Gonzague; Mère Agnès; Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur
- Source: LT 166 (to Mme Pottier, 16 July 1894), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-166-a-mme-pottier-celine-maudelonde-16-juillet-1894/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Oh ! que notre religion est belle, au lieu de rétrécir les cœurs (comme le croit le monde), elle les élève et les rend capables d'aimer »
- Translation: "Oh, how beautiful our religion is! Instead of narrowing hearts (as the world believes), it raises them and makes them able to love."
- Other sources: LT 159
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
- Notes: She catches herself using the word "trials" in a wedding letter: « Les épreuves, comment ai-je pu mettre ce mot dans ma lettre… ? » ("Trials: how could I put that word in my letter…?").
### F-OW-178 — "An iron constitution", and "never will Jesus separate us"
- Claim: Eleven days before their father's death, Thérèse wrote to Céline, who was in an inner trial about her future. "More and more I have the inner certainty that one day you will come here." Mère Marie de Gonzague told her to say so. "Here you will find more than anywhere the cross and martyrdom!" "If I die before you, do not think I will go away from your soul." Then: "I am not ill; on the contrary, I have an iron constitution, only God can break iron like clay… All that is childishness."
- When / where: 18 July 1894
- People: Céline; Mère Marie de Gonzague (novice mistress); Louis Martin (still living)
- Source: LT 167 (to Céline, 18 July 1894), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-167-a-celine-18-juillet-1894/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « je ne suis pas malade, au contraire, j'ai une santé de fer seulement le bon Dieu peut briser le fer comme l'argile... Tout cela c'est de l'enfantillage » ; « Plus je vais plus j'ai la certitude intime qu'un jour tu viendras ici, Mère M. de Gonz. me recommande de te le dire »
- Translation: "I am not ill. On the contrary, I have an iron constitution, only God can break iron like clay… All that is childishness." / "The further I go, the more I am inwardly certain that one day you will come here. Mother Marie de Gonzague tells me to tell you so."
- Other sources: 1911 moves these lines into "Lettre XIXe, 19 août 1894" (see the edit card).
- New to English readers: partial. It is in 1911, but misdated and reworded.
- Confidence: high
- Notes: A health statement in her own words, two years before the first haemoptysis. She also writes: "I went through this last year… God wanted me to make my sacrifice". The letter does not say what the sacrifice was.
### F-OW-179 — The "little hunting dog" and her novice, "the little rabbit"
- Claim: Thérèse described her role with a novice ("my daughter"). "I think she will stay. She was not brought up as we were; it is a great pity for her. Her upbringing is the cause of her unattractive manners, but at bottom she is good. Now she loves me, but I try to touch her only with white silk gloves." Thérèse had the title of "little hunting dog": the "hunters" (novice mistresses and prioresses) are too big to slip into bushes. She "licks" her "little rabbit", telling her that her fur is not smooth enough and her look too much that of a wild rabbit. "In her place I would long ago have lost myself for ever in the vast forest of the world."
- When / where: 18 July 1894, Carmel novitiate
- People: an unnamed novice ("ma fille"); the "chasseurs" (Mère Agnès as prioress and Mère Marie de Gonzague as novice mistress, not named); Céline
- Source: LT 167, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-167-a-celine-18-juillet-1894/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « elle n'a pas été élevée comme nous, c'est bien malheureux pour elle, son éducation est la cause de ses manières peu attrayantes mais dans le fond elle est bonne, maintenant elle m'aime bien, mais je tâche de ne la toucher qu'avec des gants de soie blanche... » ; « je suis un «petit chien de chasse», c'est moi qui cours après le gibier toute la journée »
- Translation: "She was not brought up as we were. It is a great pity for her. Her upbringing is the cause of her unattractive manners, but at bottom she is good. Now she is fond of me, but I try to touch her only with white silk gloves…" / "I am a 'little hunting dog'. I am the one who chases the game all day long."
- Other sources: 1911 (edited, see the next card)
- New to English readers: partial. The hunting dog is in 1911. The novice's upbringing and the "white silk gloves" are cut (ABSENT).
- Confidence: high for the words. The novice's name is not in the letter. Carmel scholarship usually identifies her as Sr Marie-Madeleine du Saint-Sacrement, but that is outside this file.
- Notes: This is the first evidence in the letters of Thérèse's work with the novices (1894). It is frank about class and upbringing.
### F-OW-180 — EDIT: 1911 turns one novice into "my little rabbits" and moves the passage to 19 Aug
- Claim: 1911 "Lettre XIXe, 19 août 1894" is a composite. It opens with a new sentence (« C'est peut-être la dernière fois… le bon Dieu a exaucé mon vœu le plus cher! Viens, nous souffrirons ensemble »). It then uses LT 167 (18 July, when Céline had not yet decided), with "Jésus ne nous séparera" changed to "le bon Dieu ne nous séparera" and "ma prophétie" added. Next comes LT 169's father passage, with "5 ans" made "cinq longues années" and "il n'y a pas un mois qu'il est au ciel" added. The hunting-dog passage follows, opened by a new line: « Depuis longtemps tu me demandes des nouvelles du noviciat, surtout des nouvelles de mon métier ». The single novice ("ma fille… mon petit lapin") becomes plural "mes petits lapins". The class remarks and "gants de soie blanche" are removed. The "hunters" become "Révérende Mère Prieure et Maîtresse des novices". "Je m'amuse" becomes "Je ris". An added line says the rabbit "you know" "a couru bien des dangers" and is worth "cent fois" more.
- When / where: originals LT 167 (18 July 1894) and LT 169 (19 Aug 1894); 1911 Lettre XIXe
- People: Mère Agnès (editor)
- Source: LT 167, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-167-a-celine-18-juillet-1894/ ; LT 169, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-169-a-celine-19-aout-1894/ ; 1911 Lettre XIXe à Céline
- Source type: own-writing compared with the edited text
- Rights: public-domain; Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: LT 167: « Je ne veux pas faire de mal à mon petit lapin » / 1911: « Aussi je veille de près mes petits lapins; je ne veux pas leur faire de mal »
- Translation: "I do not want to hurt my little rabbit" / "So I watch my little rabbits closely. I do not want to hurt them."
- Other sources: 1911 text read directly; chk.py
- New to English readers: yes (the difference)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: This is one of the clearest editorial rewrites in this period. It protects a living nun, changes the date, and makes Thérèse's role sound more official.
### F-OW-181 — Céline's two photos; "what you feel about the boys"
- Claim: Thérèse thanked Céline for "your 2 little photos, they are charming". She sent two canticles she had composed. In a P.S. she wrote: "I understand what you feel about the boys… But it is only a moment to get through, one day you will not see too many of them, cheer up!"
- When / where: 18 July 1894. The family was travelling ("chers voyageurs… là-bas").
- People: Céline; Marie Guérin; the Guérins; Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur (who addressed "mon chant d'aujourd'hui" to Mme Guérin)
- Source: LT 167, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-167-a-celine-18-juillet-1894/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Je te remercie de tes 2 petites pho., elles sont charmantes. » ; « Je comprends ce que tu ressens pour les garçons... Mais ce n'est qu'un moment à passer, un jour tu n'en verras pas trop, console-toi !... »
- Translation: "Thank you for your 2 little photos, they are charming." / "I understand what you feel about the boys… But it is only a moment to get through. One day you won't see too many of them, cheer up!…"
- Other sources: baseline (Céline's photography)
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high for the words. The letter does not say which boys, or why Céline was uneasy.
- Notes: She adds: « Oh ! que la souffrance agrandit mon âme et l'approche du terme !... » ("Oh, how suffering enlarges my soul and brings the end nearer!…"). ABSENT in 1911.
### F-OW-182 — Céline's vocation contested: "his beloved Father was wrong"
- Claim: After their father's death, Céline's entry was opposed. Thérèse wrote: "I have suffered so much for you that I hope I am not an obstacle to your vocation." She would not write "to the Father" today but would wait for his letter. "I have a lot on my heart!!!" Marie "does not hesitate to say that her beloved Father was wrong… but he was only the docile instrument of Jesus, so little Thérèse does not hold it against him!" Mère Marie de Gonzague "cried too reading your letter, poor mother, she knows nothing at all… you see how discreet we are!"
- When / where: 5–10 Aug 1894
- People: Céline; "le Père" (Marie's director, not named; likely Fr Pichon SJ from outside knowledge); Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur; Mère Marie de Gonzague; Mme Guérin
- Source: LT 168 (to Céline, 5–10 Aug 1894), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-168-a-celine-5-10-aout-1894/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Elle n'hésite pas à dire que son Père bien-aimé s'est trompé.. mais il n'a été que l'instrument docile de Jésus, aussi la petite Thérèse ne lui en veut pas !... » ; « (Mère M. de G. a bien pleuré aussi en lisant ta lettre, pauvre mère, elle ne sait rien du tout... tu vois comme nous sommes discrètes !) »
- Translation: "She does not hesitate to say that her beloved Father was mistaken… but he was only the docile instrument of Jesus, so little Thérèse does not hold it against him!…" / "(Mother Marie de Gonzague also cried a lot reading your letter, poor mother, she knows nothing at all… you see how discreet we are!)"
- Other sources: LT 169 (opposition at Caen)
- New to English readers: yes ("Tu vois comme nous sommes discrètes": ABSENT)
- Confidence: high for the words. Medium on the details. The letter does not name Canada or say what "the Father" advised.
- Notes: This is direct evidence of disagreement with a priest-director over Céline's vocation. It also shows the sisters kept information from Mère Marie de Gonzague.
### F-OW-183 — The "storm at Caen": Francis and Jeanne against Céline's entry
- Claim: Thérèse wrote her last letter to Céline "in the world". She was not surprised at "the storm that rumbles at Caen": "F. and J. have chosen a road so different from ours that they cannot understand the sublimity of our vocation!… But they laugh best who laugh last." Priests and fervent Christians think "we are exaggerated", that "we should serve with Martha". Herod accused Jesus of being mad, "and I think as he does!", for it was madness to seek poor human hearts.
- When / where: 19 Aug 1894
- People: Céline; Dr Francis La Néele and Jeanne La Néele ("F. et J."); "les prêtres" (unnamed)
- Source: LT 169 (to Céline, 19 Aug 1894), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-169-a-celine-19-aout-1894/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « F. et J. ont choisi une route si différente de la nôtre qu'ils ne peuvent comprendre la sublimité de notre vocation !... Mais riront bien qui riront les derniers... » ; « les chrétiens les plus fervents, les prêtres trouvent que nous sommes exagérées, que nous devrions servir avec Marthe »
- Translation: "F. and J. have chosen a road so different from ours that they cannot understand how sublime our vocation is!… But they laugh best who laugh last…" / "the most fervent Christians, priests, find us exaggerated, and think we should serve with Martha"
- Other sources: 1911 Lettre XXe keeps the "folly" passage but cuts F. and J. and the priests. It changes "je pense comme lui" to "et franchement c'était vrai!".
- New to English readers: yes for the opposition (ABSENT in 1911). Partial for the "folly" passage.
- Confidence: high for the words. The letter does not describe the form of the opposition.
- Notes: Documented family opposition to Céline entering a fourth sister into the same Carmel. The letter gives no reasons beyond a "different road".
### F-OW-184 — "After a death of 5 years": the father's signs after his death
- Claim: Thérèse was touched by Céline's "miraculous catch", one of the "little delicacies" that showed "our dear Father is near us". "After a death of 5 years, what joy to find him always the same, looking as before for ways to please us!" "Now that he is a pure spirit it is easy for him to go and find priests and bishops." So he had less trouble for Céline "than for his poor little queen".
- When / where: 19 Aug 1894, three weeks after Louis Martin's death (29 July 1894)
- People: Céline; Louis Martin
- Source: LT 169, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-169-a-celine-19-aout-1894/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Que ta pêche miraculeuse nous a doucement touchées... » ; « maintenant qu'il est un pur esprit ce lui est chose facile d'aller trouver les prêtres et les évêques, aussi n'a-t-il pas eu tant de mal pour sa Céline chérie que pour sa pauvre petite reine !... »
- Translation: "How sweetly your miraculous catch touched us…" / "now that he is a pure spirit it is easy for him to go and see the priests and bishops. So he did not have as much trouble for his dear Céline as for his poor little queen!…"
- Other sources: 1911 Lettre XIXe (reworded)
- New to English readers: partial. The father passage is in 1911 in edited form. "pêche miraculeuse" and "priests and bishops" are ABSENT.
- Confidence: high for her words. The letter does not say what the "pêche miraculeuse" was.
- Notes: "Une mort de 5 ans" counts from his mental collapse and admission at Caen (Feb 1889), not from his bodily death. Compare LT 170 ("6 years of absence").

- Claim: At the end of LT 169, Thérèse passed on requests. "The infirmarian would like you to find in Caen a half-bottle of Tisserand's anti-haemorrhage water, 2 F 50. If there are only whole bottles, don't take any, there is some here in Lisieux." And: "Sr Marie du S.C. would like 7 or 8 nutcrackers."

- Translation: "The infirmarian would like you to find in Caen a half-bottle of Tisserand's anti-haemorrhage water, 2.50 francs." / "Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur would like 7 or 8 nutcrackers."

### F-OW-186 — Papa's death "does not feel like a death but a true life"
- Claim: Writing to Léonie three weeks after their father's death, Thérèse said: "Papa's death does not have the effect of a death on me but of a true life. I find him again after 6 years of absence, I feel him around me looking at me and protecting me." She understood the emptiness Céline's departure would leave for Léonie. She had only a few minutes, as the post was waiting.
- When / where: 20 Aug 1894
- People: Léonie (Sr Thérèse-Dosithée); Louis Martin (d. 29 July 1894); Céline
- Source: LT 170 (to Sr Thérèse-Dosithée, 20 Aug 1894), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-170-a-soeur-therese-dosithee-leonie-20-aout-1894/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « La mort de Papa ne me fait pas l'effet d'une mort mais d'une véritable vie. Je le retrouve après 6 ans d'absence, je le sens autour de moi me regardant et me protégeant... »
- Translation: "Papa's death does not strike me as a death but as a true life. I find him again after 6 years of absence. I feel him around me, watching me and protecting me…"
- Other sources: LT 169; Ms A
- New to English readers: yes (ABSENT in 1911)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: "6 ans d'absence" counts from her entry to the Carmel (April 1888). LT 169 counts "5 years" from his collapse. There is no letter in this file written on or just after 29 July 1894.
### F-OW-187 — Céline's entry and the "sign" about her father; Sr Aimée de Jésus
- Claim: Louis Martin died on 29 Jul 1894. Céline had hidden "a secret" from Thérèse for two years (Fr Pichon's plan for her to go to Canada). Before Céline's entry, Thérèse asked God for a sign that her father had gone straight to Heaven: that "Sr A. de J." would consent to Céline's entry. This sister "found we were already too many with three" and opposed another; after Thérèse's thanksgiving she was the first person Thérèse met, called her kindly, and spoke of Céline "with tears in her eyes". Céline entered on 14 Sep 1894.
- When / where: Jul–Sep 1894, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Céline; Sr Aimée de Jésus ("Sr A. de J."); Louis Martin; Fr Pichon (implied)
- Source: Ms A 82r–82v
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « Cette soeur ... trouvait que nous étions déjà trop de trois »
- Translation: "This sister ... thought that three of us were already too many."
- Other sources: The 1911 text removes the initials and the "too many" objection. Identification of "Sr A. de J." as Sr Aimée de Jésus is the modern editors' reading; verify in other parts.
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high for the text; medium for the identification.
### F-OW-188 — Praying and sacrificing for Léonie's trials at the Visitation
- Claim: Thérèse was happy that Léonie's feast now fell on 15 Oct, like her own. "Since we have known about your trials" the sisters' fervour had been great, with prayers and sacrifices. She was confident "my dear little Visitandine" would come out victorious. Jesus sleeps while His bride fights the waves of temptation.
- When / where: 11 Oct 1894
- People: Léonie; "tes BONNES Mères" (capitals hers); St Margaret Mary
- Source: LT 171 (to Sr Thérèse-Dosithée, 11 Oct 1894), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-171-a-soeur-therese-dosithee-leonie-11-octobre-1894/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Depuis que nous connaissons tes épreuves notre ferveur est bien grande, je t'assure »
- Translation: "Since we have known about your trials our fervour has been very great, I assure you."
- Other sources: LT 165; LT 176; LT 178
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The letter does not say what the trials were.
### F-OW-189 — The "divine Beggar of love" in the Guérin home
- Claim: For her aunt's feast, Thérèse said Jesus came to rest in the Guérins' house as at Bethany: "the divine Beggar of love", who asks for more in proportion to what He receives. She quoted: "the greatest honour God can do a soul is not to give it much, but to ask much of it". She said the aunt had given "six of your children" to be His brides, "six times His Mother". She began "with my soul still perfumed by my Uncle's beautiful letter to Sr Marie-Madeleine".
- When / where: 17 Nov 1894
- People: Mme Guérin; Isidore Guérin; Sr Marie-Madeleine (a novice of the Lisieux Carmel); "Bonne-Maman" (Mme Fournet)

- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription: unknown

- Translation: "It is truly 'the divine Beggar of love' who asks for hospitality." / "you give Him six of your children to be His brides!…"
- Other sources: 1911 uses "le plus grand honneur que Dieu…" in a different, later letter.
- New to English readers: yes ("Mendiant d'amour": ABSENT)

- Notes: The letter shows that her uncle wrote to a novice (Sr Marie-Madeleine). This may link to the novice in LT 167, but the file does not confirm it.
### F-OW-211 — Books leave her dry; only the Gospel feeds her
- Claim: At 17–18 St John of the Cross was her only spiritual food; later "all books left me in aridity... If I open a book by a spiritual author (even the most beautiful), I feel my heart tighten". Scripture and the Imitation help; "above all it is the Gospel". "I have never heard him speak, but I feel he is in me"; lights come "rather in the middle of the day's occupations" than at prayer.
- When / where: 1890–1895
- People: —
- Source: Ms A 83r–83v
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: « je sens aussitôt mon coeur se serrer »
- Translation: "I immediately feel my heart tighten"
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high.
### F-OW-397 — Gifts for the clothing: a lily crown, a giant sugar stick, gingerbread for her 16th birthday
- Claim: Her cousins gave the lilies for her clothing crown; they look "just picked". She was happy to have "the enormous sugar stick" (sucre de pomme) to offer to "our Mother". The big packet of gingerbread arrived; "I was very proud in the refectory when our mother told the Community that in honour of my 16 years you were giving us this treat." The Mother and the community find the crown very beautiful. She prays to be adorned inside as well as outside.

- People: Mme Guérin; Isidore Guérin; Jeanne and Marie Guérin; Mère Marie de Gonzague ("notre Mère"); the community.

- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown

### F-OW-398 — Clothing retreat: "the creatures... there is something that repels me"
- Claim: In her clothing retreat, Thérèse tells Pauline that Jesus "riddles me with pin-pricks"; the little ball has little holes everywhere, which hurt more than one big one. With Jesus: "nothing, dryness! sleep!" But: "the creatures, oh! the creatures!... The ones around me are very good, but there is something that repels me." She is happy that Jesus sleeps: "he does not go to any trouble to make conversation with me". On her betrothal day she wants to "convert all the sinners on earth and save all the souls in purgatory"; "I know it is madness."
- When / where: 6 January 1889, Lisieux Carmel (retreat before the clothing of 10 January).
- People: Sr Agnès de Jésus; the community ("les créatures").
- Source: LT 74 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, 6 Jan 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-74-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-6-janvier-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Mais les créatures !... Celles qui m'entourent sont bien bonnes, mais il y a je ne sais quoi qui me repousse !... [...] je vous assure qu'il ne fait pas de frais pour me tenir conversation !... [...] je voudrais convertir tous les pécheurs de la terre et sauver toutes les âmes du purgatoire !... »
- Translation: "But the creatures! The ones around me are very good, but there is something I don't know what that repels me!... - New to English readers: partial — edited text in Taylor; the "repels me" line is new.

- Notes: Honest admission of difficulty with the community in her first year.

### F-OW-400 — Pain at Sr Saint-Vincent-de-Paul's; the clothing date moved from the 9th
- Claim: "This morning I had some pain at Sr Saint-Vincent-de-Paul's; I left with a heavy heart." She notes Jesus changed the date: "He no doubt found that the date of the 9th was too delightful; he wants nothing delightful for her." (Her entry had been on 9 April; the clothing was set for 10 January.) She is "deprived of all consolation" but has great peace. "The letter from the Father" (Fr Pichon) is heavenly, "but happiness? ... happiness is only in suffering, and in suffering without any consolation".

- People: Sr Agnès; Sr Saint-Vincent-de-Paul (Carmelite, not described further); Fr Pichon.
